<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that every complex <i>C</i><sup>∗</sup>-algebra in which each positive element is properly infinite is strongly purely infinite. This answers the ordinary-to-strong part of Kirchberg–Rørdam's comparison question. For exact algebras, we also prove that proper infiniteness of one fixed finite amplification of every positive element implies proper infiniteness of each positive element. Consequently, every separable nuclear algebra with this fixed-amplification property absorbs $`\mathcal O_\infty`$, without assumptions of unitality or simplicity.

- **claim-individual-to-strong** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-individual-to-strong)): Let $C$ be a complex $C^*$-algebra satisfying $\textup{(P)}$. For all $a,b\in C_+$, $c\in C$, and $\varepsilon>0$, there are $s,t\in C$ such that $$\label{eq:main-diagonalization} \\|s^*as-a\\|<\varepsilon,\qquad \\|t^*bt-b\\|<\varepsilon,\qquad \\|s^*ct\\|<\varepsilon.$$ Consequently $C$ is strongly purely infinite.
  - Explanation: Individual proper infiniteness supplies simultaneous diagonal approximation with an arbitrary mixed entry. Specializing that entry to the product of the positive inputs proves strong pure infiniteness.
- **claim-exact-amplification-removal** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-exact-infiniteness)): Let $C$ be an exact complex $C^*$-algebra. Suppose that there is one integer $n\ge1$ such that $h^{\oplus n}$ is properly infinite for every $h\in C_+$. Then every positive element of $C$ is properly infinite. Consequently $C$ is strongly purely infinite.
  - Explanation: Exactness allows a fixed amplification common to all positive elements to be removed. The resulting individual proper infiniteness also implies strong pure infiniteness.
- **claim-nuclear-absorption** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-absorption)): Let $A$ be a separable nuclear complex $C^*$-algebra. If there is one integer $n\ge1$ such that $h^{\oplus n}$ is properly infinite for every $h\in A_+$, then $$A\cong A\otimes_{\min}\mathcal O_\infty.$$
  - Explanation: For separable nuclear algebras, the fixed-amplification hypothesis leads to tensorial absorption through individual proper infiniteness, strong pure infiniteness, and the external central-sequence absorption criterion.
- **claim-elementary-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-comparison-rules)): Let $D$ be a $C^*$-algebra.* 1. *For $h\in D_+$, $$\operatorname{Her}_D(h)=D\cap e_hD^{**}e_h.$$ If $0\le u\le K v$ for $u,v\in D_+$ and $K\ge0$, then $u\in\operatorname{Her}(v)$ and $u\precsim v$. More generally, every positive element of $\operatorname{Her}(v)$ is Cuntz subequivalent to $v$.* 2. *For a rectangular algebra matrix $z$, one has $z^*z\sim_{\mathrm{Cu}}zz^*$. Cuntz subequivalence is transitive, is preserved by block sums, and is closed under norm limits in its first variable when the second variable is fixed. Every $*$-homomorphism preserves Cuntz subequivalence and equivalence, after amplification. For integers $m,n\ge1$, $d\in M_n(D)_+$, and $v\in M_m(D)_+$, stable comparison $d\precsim v$ is equivalent to the existence of $r_j\in M_{m,n}(D)$ with $r_j^*vr_j\to d$ in norm.* 3. *If $d,v$ are positive elements in a common stabilization, $d\in\operatorname{Id}(v)$, and $v$ is properly infinite, then $d\precsim v$.
  - Explanation: The comparison rules connect hereditary membership, rectangular comparison coefficients, algebraic operations, norm limits, and comparison into properly infinite ideal generators.
- **claim-cutoff-transport** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-transport)): Let $C$ be a $C^*$-algebra, let $m,n\ge1$ be integers, and let $d\in M_n(C)_+$ and $v\in M_m(C)_+$. Suppose $d\precsim v$ in $C\otimes\mathcal K$. For $\varepsilon>0$, put $h=(d-\varepsilon)_+$. There is $w\in M_{m,n}(C^{**})$ such that $$\label{eq:transport-supports} w^*w=e_h,\qquad ww^*\le e_v,$$ and $$\label{eq:transport-membership} w\operatorname{Her}_{M_n(C)}(h)\subset M_{m,n}(C),\qquad w\operatorname{Her}_{M_n(C)}(h)w^*\subset\operatorname{Her}_{M_m(C)}(v).$$ Conjugation by $w$ is an injective $*$-homomorphism on $\operatorname{Her}(h)$. For $l\in\operatorname{Her}(h)_+$, $$\label{eq:transport-cutoffs} (wlw^*-\tau)_+=w(l-\tau)_+w^*,\qquad w(l-\tau)_+w^*\sim_{\mathrm{Cu}}(l-\tau)_+\quad(\tau\ge0),$$ and $$\label{eq:transport-hereditary-image} w\operatorname{Her}(l)w^*=\operatorname{Her}(wlw^*).$$ Every representation, with matching matrix amplifications, satisfies $$\label{eq:transport-represented-norm} \\|\pi^{(m)}(wkw^*)\\|=\\|\pi^{(n)}(k)\\| \qquad(k\in\operatorname{Her}(h)).$$ The products in these formulas are algebra elements; $w$ itself is only asserted to lie in the bidual.
  - Explanation: A cutoff comparison has a common rectangular carrier in the bidual. Its products are algebra elements, and its conjugation preserves hereditary structure, cutoff comparison classes, and represented norms.
- **claim-ideal-cutoff-rules** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-ideals-cutoffs)): Let $D$ be a $C^*$-algebra.* 1. *If $J=\overline{\bigcup_\lambda J_\lambda}$ for an increasing directed family of closed ideals and $z\in J_+$, then for every $\varepsilon>0$, $(z-\varepsilon)_+$ lies in some $J_\lambda$. In particular, the ideals $\operatorname{Id}((z-\delta)_+)$, directed as $\delta\downarrow0$, densely exhaust $\operatorname{Id}(z)$.* 2. *For $u,v\in D_+$, one has $u\in\operatorname{Id}(v)$ if and only if every irreducible representation $\pi$ with $\pi(v)=0$ also satisfies $\pi(u)=0$.* 3. *If $f,g$ are continuous nonnegative functions of a positive element $a$, vanish at zero, and have the same open positivity set in $\operatorname{sp}(a)$, then $\operatorname{Her}(f(a))=\operatorname{Her}(g(a))$ and $f(a)\sim_{\mathrm{Cu}}g(a)$.
  - Explanation: Directed ideal exhaustion captures each positive cutoff in a single ideal. Irreducible representations detect ideal membership, while matching open positivity sets determine hereditary algebras and comparison equivalence.
- **claim-fixed-graph-measurements** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-graph)): Let $C$ be a $C^*$-algebra, let $a,y\in C_+$ be contractions with $a$ rich, and let $c\in\widetilde C$ be a contraction. For an integer $k\ge1$ and fixed positive $\gamma_g,\sigma_g$ satisfying <a href="#eq:graph-constants" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:graph-constants">[eq:graph-constants]</a>, there are $$X\in\operatorname{Her}((a-1/8)_+)_+,\qquad B_1,\ldots,B_k\in\operatorname{Her}((a-1/8)_+)$$ with the following property. For any contractions $$S_i\in\operatorname{Her}((X-\alpha_0)_+),\qquad 1\le i\le k,$$ one can form a rich positive contraction $p\in\operatorname{Her}(a)$ such that, for every representation $\pi$ of $C$, $\\|\pi(a)\\|>1/2$ implies $\\|\pi(p)\\|=1$. If a representation $\pi$ of $C$ satisfies $$\pi\bigl(y(1-c^*p^2c)y\bigr)=0,\qquad \\|\pi(y)\\|>s,$$ then, in that same representation, $$\label{eq:graph-failure} \\|\pi(X)\\|>s,\qquad \pi(B_i)=\pi(S_i)\quad(1\le i\le k).$$ The elements $X,B_1,\ldots,B_k$ are fixed before the $S_i$ are chosen.
  - Explanation: The graph construction fixes its measurements before choosing its contractive entries. A vanishing defect then forces those entries to equal the fixed measurements in the same representation.
- **claim-conditional-support-shrinking** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-conditional-shrinking)): Let $C$ be a $C^*$-algebra, let $a,y\in C_+$ be contractions with $a$ rich, and let $c\in\widetilde C$ be a contraction. Suppose $p\in\operatorname{Her}(a)$ is a rich positive contraction satisfying $$\\|\pi(a)\\|>1/2\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\\|\pi(p)\\|=1 \qquad\text{for every representation }\pi\text{ of }C.$$ Put $d=y(1-c^*p^2c)y\in C_+$, and assume $$\label{eq:common-nonvanishing} \pi(d)=0\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\\|\pi(y)\\|\le\frac14 \qquad\text{for every representation }\pi\text{ of }C.$$ Then there are positive contractions $$p_1\in\operatorname{Her}(a),\qquad q_1\in\operatorname{Her}(y)$$ such that $p_1$ is rich, $$\label{eq:shrinking-signals} \begin{split} \\|\pi(a)\\|>1/2&\ \Longrightarrow\ \\|\pi(p_1)\\|=1,\\ \\|\pi(y)\\|>1/2&\ \Longrightarrow\ \\|\pi(q_1)\\|=1 \end{split} \qquad\text{for every representation }\pi\text{ of }C,$$ and $$\label{eq:shrinking-support} \\|e_{p_1}c e_{q_1}\\|_{C^{**}}<1.$$ The last inequality is a single norm bound in the universal bidual.
  - Explanation: A rich graph output and a defect nonvanishing implication yield hereditary contractions that retain the represented norm signals and strictly compress the mixed entry in the universal bidual.
- **claim-actual-range-cutoff** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-actual-range)): Let $x,Q$ be bounded positive operators on a Hilbert space $\mathcal H$, and put $U=xQ^2x$. For every $\eta>0$, $$\label{eq:actual-range} e_{(U-\eta)_+}\mathcal H\subset\operatorname{ran}(xQ), \qquad \ker Q\cap\operatorname{ran}(xQ)=\{0\}.$$
  - Explanation: A positive spectral cutoff lies in the actual range of the operator product. Positivity makes that range intersect the kernel of the second factor only trivially.
- **claim-four-entry-avoidance** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-four-avoidance)): Suppose $C$ satisfies $\textup{(P)}$. Let $X\in C_+$ and $B_1,B_2,B_3,B_4\in C$. With $$\alpha_0=\frac1{32}<\alpha=\frac1{16} <\beta=\frac18<s=\frac14,\qquad h=\frac12,$$ there exist contractions $T_1,\ldots,T_4$ in $\operatorname{Her}((X-\alpha_0)_+)$ for which no representation $\pi$ of $C$ satisfies $$\label{eq:four-forbidden} \\|\pi(X)\\|>s,\qquad \pi(B_i)=\pi(T_i)\quad(1\le i\le4).$$
  - Explanation: Under individual proper infiniteness, contractive entries can be chosen in the specified spectral hereditary algebra so that no representation both detects the positive input and matches all fixed measurements.
- **claim-shrinking-under-individual-infiniteness** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-shrinking-p)): Suppose $C$ satisfies $\textup{(P)}$. Let $a,y\in C$ be positive contractions and let $c\in\widetilde C$ be a contraction. There are positive contractions $p_1\in\operatorname{Her}(a)$ and $q_1\in\operatorname{Her}(y)$, with $p_1$ rich, such that for every representation $\pi$ of $C$, $$\label{eq:shrinking-P-signals} \\|\pi(a)\\|>1/2\ \Longrightarrow\ \\|\pi(p_1)\\|=1, \qquad \\|\pi(y)\\|>1/2\ \Longrightarrow\ \\|\pi(q_1)\\|=1,$$ and, in the universal bidual, $$\label{eq:shrinking-P-support} \\|e_{p_1}c e_{q_1}\\|<1.$$
  - Explanation: Applying measurement avoidance to the graph establishes the defect nonvanishing condition and hence strict support compression with simultaneous represented norm retention.
- **claim-bounded-product-permanence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-product-p)): Let $(C_i)_{i\in I}$ be any family of $C^*$-algebras satisfying $\textup{(P)}$. Their bounded product $D=\prod_{i\in I}C_i$ satisfies $\textup{(P)}$. More precisely, for a positive contraction $d=(d_i)\in D$ and $0<\delta<1/2$, there is a row $s=(s_i)\in M_{1,2}(D)$ such that $$\label{eq:product-row} s^*ds=(d-2\delta)_+\oplus(d-2\delta)_+,\qquad \\|s\\|\le\delta^{-1/2}.$$
  - Explanation: Individual proper infiniteness passes to bounded products because cutoff comparison rows have a common norm bound across all coordinates.
- **claim-universal-support-contraction** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-uniform-shrinking)): There exists $\theta\in(0,1)$ such that for every $C^*$-algebra $C$ satisfying $\textup{(P)}$, every pair of positive contractions $a,y\in C$, and every contraction $c\in C$, there are positive contractions $p\in\operatorname{Her}(a)$, $q\in\operatorname{Her}(y)$ such that, for every representation $\pi$ of $C$, $$\\|\pi(a)\\|>1/2\ \Longrightarrow\ \\|\pi(p)\\|=1, \qquad \\|\pi(y)\\|>1/2\ \Longrightarrow\ \\|\pi(q)\\|=1,$$ and $$\label{eq:uniform-support} \\|e_p c e_q\\|\le\theta.$$
  - Explanation: A bounded-product contradiction upgrades input-dependent strict compression to a universal contraction factor. The mixed input belongs to the algebra itself.
- **claim-iterated-support-separation** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-iterated-separation)): Suppose $C$ satisfies $\textup{(P)}$. For positive contractions $a_*,b_*\in C$, a contraction $c_*\in C$, and $\zeta>0$, there are positive contractions $p\in\operatorname{Her}(a_*)$, $q\in\operatorname{Her}(b_*)$ such that $$\\|e_p c_*e_q\\|<\zeta$$ and, for every representation $\pi$, $$\\|\pi(a_*)\\|>1/2\ \Longrightarrow\ \\|\pi(p)\\|=1, \qquad \\|\pi(b_*)\\|>1/2\ \Longrightarrow\ \\|\pi(q)\\|=1.$$
  - Explanation: Repeated normalized support shrinking makes the mixed compression arbitrarily small while nested hereditary membership and represented norm signals persist.
- **claim-bounded-arbitrary-entry-diagonalization** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-bounded-diagonalization)): Suppose $C$ satisfies $\textup{(P)}$. Let $a,b\in C_+$ be contractions and let $c\in C$. Given $$0<\delta<1/2,\qquad 0<\rho\le2\delta,\qquad \zeta>0,$$ there exist $s,t\in C$ such that $$\begin{aligned} \\|s^*as-a\\|,\ \\|t^*bt-b\\|&<2\delta+\rho, \label{eq:bounded-diagonals}\\ \\|s^*ct\\|&<\zeta,\qquad \\|s\\|,\ \\|t\\|\le\delta^{-1/2}. \label{eq:bounded-cross} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: The comparison coefficients are bounded using diagonal cutoffs before implementing the already separated corners. Their bound is independent of the requested mixed-entry accuracy.
- **claim-nuclear-amplification-removal** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-nuclear-to-individual)): Let $C$ be a nuclear complex $C^*$-algebra. Suppose that one integer $n\ge1$, fixed for the whole algebra, makes $h^{\oplus n}$ properly infinite for every $h\in C_+$. Then every positive element of $C$ is properly infinite.
  - Explanation: The independent nuclear route removes the common amplification by obtaining local rich elements, merging their representation signals, and assembling each positive cutoff into a rich element's ideal.
- **claim-forbidden-operator-tuple** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-forbidden-tuple)): There are $\ell\ge1$, a $C^*$-algebra $W$, contractions $z_1,\ldots,z_\ell\in W$, and contractions $z_j^{(r)}\in M_{N_r}(\mathbb C)$, where $N_r\ge1$, $r\ge1$, and $1\le j\le\ell$, with the following properties.* 1. *No tuple $b_1,\ldots,b_\ell$ in any exact $C^*$-algebra satisfies $$\label{eq:forbidden-tuple} \left\\|\sum_{j=1}^{\ell} A_j\otimes b_j\right\\| =\left\\|\sum_{j=1}^{\ell} A_j\otimes z_j\right\\| \quad\text{for every }m\ge1\text{ and every }A_j\in M_m(\mathbb C).$$* 2. *For every fixed $m\ge1$ and $A_j\in M_m(\mathbb C)$, $$\label{eq:tuple-approximations} \lim_{r\to\infty} \left\\|\sum_{j=1}^{\ell} A_j\otimes z_j^{(r)}\right\\| =\left\\|\sum_{j=1}^{\ell} A_j\otimes z_j\right\\|.$$
  - Explanation: A finite tuple from a nonexact algebra has joint linear matrix norms that cannot all be realized in an exact algebra. Finite compressions approximate each fixed test pointwise.
- **claim-nuclear-measurement-avoidance** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-nuclear-avoidance)): Let $C$ be nuclear and satisfy $\textup{(F\ensuremath{_n})}$ for a fixed $n\ge1$, and let $\ell$ be as in Lemma <a href="#lem:forbidden-tuple" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:forbidden-tuple">18</a>. For $X\in C_+$ and fixed elements $B_{j,pq}\in C$, where $1\le j\le\ell$ and $1\le p,q\le n$, there are contractions $$S_{j,pq}\in\operatorname{Her}((X-\alpha_0)_+)$$ such that no representation $\pi$ satisfies simultaneously $$\label{eq:nuclear-forbidden} \\|\pi(X)\\|>s,\qquad \pi(B_{j,pq})=\pi(S_{j,pq}) \quad(1\le j\le\ell,\ 1\le p,q\le n).$$ Here $\alpha_0=1/32$ and $s=1/4$, as in Proposition <a href="#prop:graph" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:graph">8</a>.
  - Explanation: Transporting each compressed tuple through a common carrier makes failure of every entry choice realize the forbidden joint matrix norms in a nuclear quotient. This contradiction supplies an avoiding choice.
- **claim-nuclear-support-shrinking** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-shrinking-nuclear)): Let $C$ be nuclear and satisfy $\textup{(F\ensuremath{_n})}$ for one fixed $n\ge1$. Let $a,y\in C_+$ be contractions, with $a$ rich, and let $c\in\widetilde C$ be a contraction. There are positive contractions $p_1\in\operatorname{Her}(a)$ and $q_1\in\operatorname{Her}(y)$ such that $p_1$ is rich, $$\label{eq:nuclear-shrinking-signals} \begin{split} \\|\pi(a)\\|>1/2&\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\\|\pi(p_1)\\|=1,\\ \\|\pi(y)\\|>1/2&\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\\|\pi(q_1)\\|=1 \end{split} \qquad\text{for every representation }\pi,$$ and $\\|e_{p_1}ce_{q_1}\\|_{C^{**}}<1$.
  - Explanation: The nuclear tuple obstruction supplies the graph's nonvanishing requirement. Conditional shrinking then retains both represented norm signals, with richness required only for the first output.
- **claim-local-rich-witness** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-local-rich)): Let $C$ be a $C^*$-algebra, and suppose that one integer $n\ge1$ makes $h^{\oplus n}$ properly infinite for every $h\in C_+$. If $u\in C_+$ and an irreducible representation $\pi:C\to B(\mathcal H)$ satisfies $\pi(u)\ne0$, then there is a rich positive contraction $p\in\operatorname{Her}(u)$ with $\\|\pi(p)\\|=1$.
  - Explanation: An irreducible representation detecting a positive input also detects a rich contraction in its hereditary algebra. The witness depends on the representation and uses the common amplification hypothesis.
- **claim-rich-witness-merger** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-merger)): Let $C$ be nuclear and satisfy $\textup{(F\ensuremath{_n})}$ for a fixed $n\ge1$. For $u\in C_+$ and rich positive contractions $a,b\in\operatorname{Her}(u)$, there is a rich positive contraction $c_0\in\operatorname{Her}(u)$ such that every representation $\pi$ satisfies $$\label{eq:merger-signal} \bigl(\\|\pi(a)\\|>1/2\ \text{or}\ \\|\pi(b)\\|>1/2\bigr) \quad\Longrightarrow\quad\\|\pi(c_0)\\|=1.$$
  - Explanation: The merger combines the represented norm signals of rich hereditary contractions into a single rich contraction. Orthogonal transported copies preserve richness even when intermediate second outputs are not rich.
- **claim-uniform-exactness-gap** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-uniform-gap)): There is a constant $\gamma>0$ such that no unital exact $C^*$-algebra $B$ contains a contraction $v$ with the following property: for every integer $d\geq1$ and every contraction $D\in M_d(\mathbb C)$, there are a unital representation $\pi:B\to B(H_\pi)$ and an isometry $J:\mathbb C^d\to H_\pi$ satisfying $$\label{eq:forbidden-models} \\|\pi(v)J-JD\\|<\gamma, \qquad \\|\pi(v)^*J-JD^*\\|<\gamma.$$ The representation and the isometry are allowed to depend on $d$ and $D$.
  - Explanation: A universal positive error threshold obstructs approximately reducing copies of all finite matrix contractions inside any fixed contraction in a unital exact algebra. Both the contraction and its adjoint must use the same copy.
- **claim-universal-contraction-nonexactness** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-lem-nonexact-universal)): The universal contraction algebra $\mathcal U$ is not exact.
  - Explanation: The universal unital algebra of a contraction is nonexact. The proof embeds the full free-group algebra and exhibits its tensor quotient obstruction.
- **claim-polynomial-finite-matrix-tests** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-matrix-tests)): For every integer $r\geq1$ and every $Z\in M_r(\mathcal U)$ whose entries are $*$-polynomials in $u,u^*$, $$\label{eq:finite-matrix-tests} \\|Z\\|_{M_r(\mathcal U)} =\sup_{d\geq1,\ D\in M_d(\mathbb C),\ \\|D\\|\leq1}\\|Z(D,D^*)\\|.$$
  - Explanation: Universal polynomial matrix norms are computed by the supremum over all finite matrix contractions. Finite-word compression preserves the tested words on the chosen vector.
- **claim-all-amplification-polynomial-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-uniform-matrix-bound)): Let $E\subset\mathcal U$ be a finite-dimensional linear space consisting of $*$-polynomials in $u,u^*$. There is a finite constant $C_E\geq0$ with the following property. Suppose $v$ is a contraction in a unital $C^*$-algebra $B$, and fix one number $\theta>0$. Assume that for every integer $d\geq1$ and every contraction $D\in M_d(\mathbb C)$, there are a unital representation $\pi:B\to B(H_\pi)$ and an isometry $J:\mathbb C^d\to H_\pi$ satisfying the two inequalities in <a href="#eq:forbidden-models" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:forbidden-models">[eq:forbidden-models]</a> with $\theta$ in place of $\gamma$. For the homomorphism $\Phi:\mathcal U\to B$ with $\Phi(u)=v$, one then has $$\label{eq:all-amplification-bound} \\|\Phi^{(r)}(Z)\\|\geq(1-C_E\theta)\\|Z\\| \qquad (r\geq1,\ Z\in M_r(E)).$$
  - Explanation: For a fixed polynomial space, approximate intertwining controls evaluation from below at every matrix amplification with the same constant. The constant depends on the polynomial space rather than on the tested matrix size.
- **claim-fixed-amplification-ideal-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-ideal-comparison)): Let $C$ be a $C^*$-algebra, let $h\in C_+$, and let $n\geq1$ be an integer such that $h^{\oplus n}$ is properly infinite. For every $x\in\operatorname{Id}(h)_+$ and every integer $k\geq1$, $$x^{\oplus k}\precsim h^{\oplus n}. \label{eq:ideal-multiplicity}$$ In particular, if $C$ satisfies <span class="upright">(F$_n$)</span>, this conclusion holds for every $h\in C_+$ with the same $n$.
  - Explanation: Every finite amplification of a positive element in the generated ideal compares into the properly infinite fixed amplification of the generator.
- **claim-exact-cut-implementation** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-cut-implementation)): Let $C$ be a $C^*$-algebra, let $m,s\geq1$, and suppose that $x\in M_m(C)_+$ and $h\in M_s(C)_+$ satisfy $x\precsim h$. If $f\colon[0,\\|x\\|]\to[0,\infty)$ is continuous and vanishes on a neighborhood of zero, then there is $R\in M_{s,m}(C)$ such that $$R^*R=f(x),\qquad RR^*\in\operatorname{Her}(h),\qquad \\|R\\|^2=\\|f(x)\\|. \label{eq:exact-cut}$$
  - Explanation: A continuous positive function vanishing near zero admits an exact rectangular implementation when its input compares to the target. The range square belongs to the target hereditary algebra.
- **claim-exact-matrix-unitization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-exact-matrix-unitization)): If $A$ is an exact $C^*$-algebra and $n\ge1$, then $\widetilde{M_n(A)}$ is exact.
  - Explanation: Finite matrix amplification and minimal unitization preserve exactness, providing the unital exact target required by the matrix-model obstruction.
- **claim-uniform-primitive-perturbation** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-perturbation)): Let $C$ be an exact $C^*$-algebra satisfying <span class="upright">(F$_n$)</span> for one fixed integer $n\geq1$. Let $p,q\in C$ be contractions and let $h,k,c\in C_+$ satisfy $$p^*ph=h,\qquad qq^*k=k,\qquad c^{\oplus n}\precsim h,\qquad c^{\oplus n}\precsim k. \label{eq:perturbation-hypotheses}$$ For every $T\in C$ and every $\eta,\alpha>0$, there is a contraction $y\in C$ such that $$\\|(T+\eta pyq)_P\\|\geq\sigma\eta \quad\text{for every }P\in\operatorname{Prim}(C)\text{ with }\\|c_P\\|\geq\alpha. \label{eq:perturbation-conclusion}$$ If $h,k$ belong to a hereditary subalgebra $\mathcal D\subset C$, then $y$ may be chosen in $\mathcal D$. If, in addition or independently, $p,q$ are positive and $pq=0$, then $y$ may be chosen selfadjoint. Both conclusions may be imposed simultaneously. The constant $\sigma$ is independent of all the data in the Proposition, including $n$ and the norms of $T,h,k,c$.
  - Explanation: A contractive perturbation simultaneously prevents cancellation on every primitive quotient in the prescribed norm slice. Hereditary membership and selfadjointness can be retained under the stated additional conditions.
- **claim-local-orthogonal-common-elements** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-local-common-element)): Let $C$ be an exact $C^*$-algebra satisfying <span class="upright">(F$_n$)</span> for one fixed integer $n\geq1$, and let $P\in\operatorname{Prim}(C)$.* 1. *If $h\in C_+$ and $h_P\ne0$, then for every integer $m\ge1$ there are $m$ pairwise orthogonal elements of $\operatorname{Her}(h)_+$, each nonzero at $P$.* 2. *If $h,k\in C_+$ are nonzero at $P$, there is $c\in C_+$ such that $c_P\ne0$ and $$c^{\oplus n}\precsim h,\qquad c^{\oplus n}\precsim k.$$ If $h,k$ belong to a hereditary subalgebra $\mathcal D$, $c$ can also be chosen in $\mathcal D$.
  - Explanation: At a primitive quotient detecting the input, hereditary algebras contain arbitrarily long orthogonal detected lists. A common positive element can be chosen whose fixed amplification compares into both prescribed targets.
- **claim-irreducible-norm-attainment** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-purestateattainment)): If $B$ is a $C^*$-algebra and $0\ne b\in B_+$, there is an irreducible representation $\pi$ of $B$ with $\\|\pi(b)\\|=\\|b\\|$.
  - Explanation: A nonzero positive element attains its norm in an irreducible representation. This supplies strict quotient norm control in the primitive-cover argument.
- **claim-quasicompact-primitive-norm-slices** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-primitive-compactness)): Let $C$ be a $C^*$-algebra. For $c\in C_+$ and $\alpha>0$, the set $$\Omega(c,\alpha)=\{P\in\operatorname{Prim}(C):\\|c_P\\|>\alpha\}$$ is open, and $$K(c,\alpha)=\{P\in\operatorname{Prim}(C):\\|c_P\\|\ge\alpha\}$$ is quasi-compact. No Hausdorff hypothesis is required.
  - Explanation: Strict primitive norm slices are open, and non-strict slices are quasi-compact without a Hausdorff assumption. Quasi-compactness does not assert that the slice is closed.
- **claim-finite-primitive-coupling** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-coupling)): Let $C$ be an exact $C^*$-algebra satisfying <span class="upright">(F$_n$)</span> for one fixed integer $n\geq1$. Let $K_0\subset\operatorname{Prim}(C)$ be quasi-compact and let $\mathcal D\subset C$ be hereditary. Suppose that at each $P\in K_0$ one can choose positive contractions $p,q\in\mathcal D$ and $h,k\in\mathcal D_+$ satisfying $$pq=0,\qquad ph=h,\qquad qk=k,\qquad h_P\ne0,\quad k_P\ne0.$$ There are finitely many such choices $(p_i,q_i,h_i,k_i)$ and one selfadjoint $U\in\mathcal D$ such that, at every $P\in K_0$, $$(p_iUq_i)_P\ne0$$ for at least one selected index $i$. Any support conditions in the original choices are retained in the selected ones.
  - Explanation: A finite selection of locally available orthogonal corners can be coupled by a common selfadjoint element throughout a quasi-compact primitive subset. Later perturbations are small enough to preserve earlier nonzero corners.
- **claim-quasicompact-square-zero-localization** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-square-zero)): Let $C$ be an exact $C^*$-algebra satisfying <span class="upright">(F$_n$)</span> for one fixed integer $n\geq1$. Let $K_0\subset\operatorname{Prim}(C)$ be quasi-compact, and let $\mathcal D\subset C$ be a hereditary subalgebra nonzero at every point of $K_0$. There exists $x\in\mathcal D$ such that $$x^2=0,\qquad x_P\ne0\quad(P\in K_0).$$
  - Explanation: A hereditary algebra nonzero throughout a quasi-compact primitive subset contains a square-zero element nonzero throughout that same subset. The construction couples spectral windows and then the positive and negative parts.
- **claim-whole-ideal-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-ideal-comparison)): Let $C$ be a $C^*$-algebra satisfying <span class="upright">(P)</span>. For every $h\in C_+$, $c\in\operatorname{Id}(h)_+$, and integer $k\ge1$, $$c^{\oplus k}\precsim h.$$ If $C$ is exact, Proposition <a href="#prop:perturbation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:perturbation">31</a> may be applied with multiplicity $n=1$, with the same constant $\sigma$ and all its stated options.
  - Explanation: Under individual proper infiniteness, every finite amplification of a positive element in the whole generated ideal compares to its generator. Exactness additionally permits the uniform perturbation result without retaining a larger multiplicity.
- **claim-fixed-floor-positive-separation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-separating-positives)): Let $C$ be an exact $C^*$-algebra satisfying $\textup{(P)}$, and let $0<\sigma<1$ be the constant in Proposition <a href="#prop:perturbation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:perturbation">31</a> with $n=1$. Let $a,b\in C_+$ be contractions, and let $0<r<1$. Set $$m=\frac{r\sigma^2}{1024}.$$ For every $0<\ell<1/10$ there are positives $Q_a\in\operatorname{Her}(a)$ and $Q_b\in\operatorname{Her}(b)$ such that $$\begin{aligned} \\|(Q_a)_P\\|&\ge m &&\text{if }\\|a_P\\|\ge r,\notag\\ \\|(Q_b)_P\\|&\ge m &&\text{if }\\|b_P\\|\ge r, \label{eq:separation-quotient-bounds}\\ \\|Q_aQ_b\\|&\le20\sqrt\ell. \label{eq:separation-product-bound} \end{aligned}$$ Here and below $P$ ranges over $\operatorname{Prim}(C)$.
  - Explanation: The separated hereditary positives keep a common lower norm floor on their prescribed primitive slices while their product becomes small. The floor is chosen before the product accuracy.
- **claim-spectral-floor-bounded-implementation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-bounded-implementation)): Let $C$ be a $C^*$-algebra, let $Q,c\in C_+$ with $\\|c\\|\le1$, and let $m>0$. If $c\precsim(Q^2-m^2/4)_+$, then for each $0<\delta<1$ there is $x\in C$ such that $$\label{eq:separation-bounded-implementation} \\|(Qx)^*(Qx)-c\\|<\delta,\qquad \\|Qx\\|\le2,\qquad \\|x\\|\le\frac4m.$$
  - Explanation: Comparison into a cutoff above a positive spectral floor can be implemented with coefficient bounds depending on that floor rather than on the original comparison coefficient.
- **claim-direct-exact-strong-infiniteness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-direct-strong-infiniteness)): Let $C$ be an exact $C^*$-algebra satisfying $\textup{(P)}$. For every $a,b\in C_+$ and every $\varepsilon>0$, there are $s,t\in C$ such that $$\\|s^*a^2s-a^2\\|<\varepsilon,\qquad \\|t^*b^2t-b^2\\|<\varepsilon,\qquad \\|s^*abt\\|<\varepsilon.$$ Consequently $C$ is strongly purely infinite.
  - Explanation: The direct exact route combines fixed-floor positive separation with bounded comparison implementation to establish the defining strong-infiniteness inequalities.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-comparison-rules",
        "exact_quote": "Let $D$ be a $C^*$-algebra.*\n\n1.  *For $h\\in D_+$, $$\\operatorname{Her}_D(h)=D\\cap e_hD^{**}e_h.$$ If $0\\le u\\le K v$ for $u,v\\in D_+$ and $K\\ge0$, then $u\\in\\operatorname{Her}(v)$ and $u\\precsim v$. More generally, every positive element of $\\operatorname{Her}(v)$ is Cuntz subequivalent to $v$.*\n\n2.  *For a rectangular algebra matrix $z$, one has $z^*z\\sim_{\\mathrm{Cu}}zz^*$. Cuntz subequivalence is transitive, is preserved by block sums, and is closed under norm limits in its first variable when the second variable is fixed. Every $*$-homomorphism preserves Cuntz subequivalence and equivalence, after amplification. For integers $m,n\\ge1$, $d\\in M_n(D)_+$, and $v\\in M_m(D)_+$, stable comparison $d\\precsim v$ is equivalent to the existence of $r_j\\in M_{m,n}(D)$ with $r_j^*vr_j\\to d$ in norm.*\n\n3.  *If $d,v$ are positive elements in a common stabilization, $d\\in\\operatorname{Id}(v)$, and $v$ is properly infinite, then $d\\precsim v$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-transport",
        "exact_quote": "Let $C$ be a $C^*$-algebra, let $m,n\\ge1$ be integers, and let $d\\in M_n(C)_+$ and $v\\in M_m(C)_+$. Suppose $d\\precsim v$ in $C\\otimes\\mathcal K$. For $\\varepsilon>0$, put $h=(d-\\varepsilon)_+$. There is $w\\in M_{m,n}(C^{**})$ such that $$\\label{eq:transport-supports}\n w^*w=e_h,\\qquad ww^*\\le e_v,$$ and $$\\label{eq:transport-membership}\n w\\operatorname{Her}_{M_n(C)}(h)\\subset M_{m,n}(C),\\qquad\n w\\operatorname{Her}_{M_n(C)}(h)w^*\\subset\\operatorname{Her}_{M_m(C)}(v).$$ Conjugation by $w$ is an injective $*$-homomorphism on $\\operatorname{Her}(h)$. For $l\\in\\operatorname{Her}(h)_+$, $$\\label{eq:transport-cutoffs}\n (wlw^*-\\tau)_+=w(l-\\tau)_+w^*,\\qquad\n w(l-\\tau)_+w^*\\sim_{\\mathrm{Cu}}(l-\\tau)_+\\quad(\\tau\\ge0),$$ and $$\\label{eq:transport-hereditary-image}\n w\\operatorname{Her}(l)w^*=\\operatorname{Her}(wlw^*).$$ Every representation, with matching matrix amplifications, satisfies $$\\label{eq:transport-represented-norm}\n \\|\\pi^{(m)}(wkw^*)\\|=\\|\\pi^{(n)}(k)\\|\n \\qquad(k\\in\\operatorname{Her}(h)).$$ The products in these formulas are algebra elements; $w$ itself is only asserted to lie in the bidual.",
        "source_id": "PM-303.1"
      },

For $l\\in\\operatorname{Her}(h)_+$, $$\\label{eq:transport-cutoffs}\n (wlw^*-\\tau)_+=w(l-\\tau)_+w^*,\\qquad\n w(l-\\tau)_+w^*\\sim_{\\mathrm{Cu}}(l-\\tau)_+\\quad(\\tau\\ge0),$$ and $$\\label{eq:transport-hereditary-image}\n w\\operatorname{Her}(l)w^*=\\operatorname{Her}(wlw^*).$$ Every representation, with matching matrix amplifications, satisfies $$\\label{eq:transport-represented-norm}\n \\|\\pi^{(m)}(wkw^*)\\|=\\|\\pi^{(n)}(k)\\|\n \\qquad(k\\in\\operatorname{Her}(h)).$$ The products in these formulas are algebra elements; $w$ itself is only asserted to lie in the bidual.",
          "source_id": "PM-303.1"

For $l\\in\\operatorname{Her}(h)_+$, $$\\label{eq:transport-cutoffs}\n (wlw^*-\\tau)_+=w(l-\\tau)_+w^*,\\qquad\n w(l-\\tau)_+w^*\\sim_{\\mathrm{Cu}}(l-\\tau)_+\\quad(\\tau\\ge0),$$ and $$\\label{eq:transport-hereditary-image}\n w\\operatorname{Her}(l)w^*=\\operatorname{Her}(wlw^*).$$ Every representation, with matching matrix amplifications, satisfies $$\\label{eq:transport-represented-norm}\n \\|\\pi^{(m)}(wkw^*)\\|=\\|\\pi^{(n)}(k)\\|\n \\qquad(k\\in\\operatorname{Her}(h)).$$ The products in these formulas are algebra elements; $w$ itself is only asserted to lie in the bidual.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-ideals-cutoffs",
        "exact_quote": "Let $D$ be a $C^*$-algebra.*\n\n1.  *If $J=\\overline{\\bigcup_\\lambda J_\\lambda}$ for an increasing directed family of closed ideals and $z\\in J_+$, then for every $\\varepsilon>0$, $(z-\\varepsilon)_+$ lies in some $J_\\lambda$. In particular, the ideals $\\operatorname{Id}((z-\\delta)_+)$, directed as $\\delta\\downarrow0$, densely exhaust $\\operatorname{Id}(z)$.*\n\n2.  *For $u,v\\in D_+$, one has $u\\in\\operatorname{Id}(v)$ if and only if every irreducible representation $\\pi$ with $\\pi(v)=0$ also satisfies $\\pi(u)=0$.*\n\n3.  *If $f,g$ are continuous nonnegative functions of a positive element $a$, vanish at zero, and have the same open positivity set in $\\operatorname{sp}(a)$, then $\\operatorname{Her}(f(a))=\\operatorname{Her}(g(a))$ and $f(a)\\sim_{\\mathrm{Cu}}g(a)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-303.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-ideals-cutoffs",

In particular, the ideals $\\operatorname{Id}((z-\\delta)_+)$, directed as $\\delta\\downarrow0$, densely exhaust $\\operatorname{Id}(z)$.*\n\n2.  *For $u,v\\in D_+$, one has $u\\in\\operatorname{Id}(v)$ if and only if every irreducible representation $\\pi$ with $\\pi(v)=0$ also satisfies $\\pi(u)=0$.*\n\n3.  *If $f,g$ are continuous nonnegative functions of a positive element $a$, vanish at zero, and have the same open positivity set in $\\operatorname{sp}(a)$, then $\\operatorname{Her}(f(a))=\\operatorname{Her}(g(a))$ and $f(a)\\sim_{\\mathrm{Cu}}g(a)$.",
          "source_id": "PM-303.1"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-graph",
        "exact_quote": "Let $C$ be a $C^*$-algebra, let $a,y\\in C_+$ be contractions with $a$ rich, and let $c\\in\\widetilde C$ be a contraction. For an integer $k\\ge1$ and fixed positive $\\gamma_g,\\sigma_g$ satisfying <a href=\"#eq:graph-constants\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:graph-constants\">[eq:graph-constants]</a>, there are $$X\\in\\operatorname{Her}((a-1/8)_+)_+,\\qquad\n B_1,\\ldots,B_k\\in\\operatorname{Her}((a-1/8)_+)$$ with the following property. For any contractions $$S_i\\in\\operatorname{Her}((X-\\alpha_0)_+),\\qquad 1\\le i\\le k,$$ one can form a rich positive contraction $p\\in\\operatorname{Her}(a)$ such that, for every representation $\\pi$ of $C$, $\\|\\pi(a)\\|>1/2$ implies $\\|\\pi(p)\\|=1$. If a representation $\\pi$ of $C$ satisfies $$\\pi\\bigl(y(1-c^*p^2c)y\\bigr)=0,\\qquad \\|\\pi(y)\\|>s,$$ then, in that same representation, $$\\label{eq:graph-failure}\n \\|\\pi(X)\\|>s,\\qquad \\pi(B_i)=\\pi(S_i)\\quad(1\\le i\\le k).$$ The elements $X,B_1,\\ldots,B_k$ are fixed before the $S_i$ are chosen.",
        "source_id": "PM-303.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-graph",
          "exact_quote": "Let $C$ be a $C^*$-algebra, let $a,y\\in C_+$ be contractions with $a$ rich, and let $c\\in\\widetilde C$ be a contraction. For any contractions $$S_i\\in\\operatorname{Her}((X-\\alpha_0)_+),\\qquad 1\\le i\\le k,$$ one can form a rich positive contraction $p\\in\\operatorname{Her}(a)$ such that, for every representation $\\pi$ of $C$, $\\|\\pi(a)\\|>1/2$ implies $\\|\\pi(p)\\|=1$. If a representation $\\pi$ of $C$ satisfies $$\\pi\\bigl(y(1-c^*p^2c)y\\bigr)=0,\\qquad \\|\\pi(y)\\|>s,$$ then, in that same representation, $$\\label{eq:graph-failure}\n \\|\\pi(X)\\|>s,\\qquad \\pi(B_i)=\\pi(S_i)\\quad(1\\le i\\le k).$$ The elements $X,B_1,\\ldots,B_k$ are fixed before the $S_i$ are chosen.",
          "source_id": "PM-303.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $C$ be a $C^*$-algebra, let $a,y\\in C_+$ be contractions with $a$ rich, and let $c\\in\\widetilde C$ be a contraction. If a representation $\\pi$ of $C$ satisfies $$\\pi\\bigl(y(1-c^*p^2c)y\\bigr)=0,\\qquad \\|\\pi(y)\\|>s,$$ then, in that same representation, $$\\label{eq:graph-failure}\n \\|\\pi(X)\\|>s,\\qquad \\pi(B_i)=\\pi(S_i)\\quad(1\\le i\\le k).$$ The elements $X,B_1,\\ldots,B_k$ are fixed before the $S_i$ are chosen.",
      "explanation": "The graph construction fixes its measurements before choosing its contractive entries. A vanishing defect then forces those entries to equal the fixed measurements in the same representation.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-conditional-shrinking",
        "exact_quote": "Let $C$ be a $C^*$-algebra, let $a,y\\in C_+$ be contractions with $a$ rich, and let $c\\in\\widetilde C$ be a contraction. Suppose $p\\in\\operatorname{Her}(a)$ is a rich positive contraction satisfying $$\\|\\pi(a)\\|>1/2\\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\\|\\pi(p)\\|=1\n \\qquad\\text{for every representation }\\pi\\text{ of }C.$$ Put $d=y(1-c^*p^2c)y\\in C_+$, and assume $$\\label{eq:common-nonvanishing}\n \\pi(d)=0\\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\\|\\pi(y)\\|\\le\\frac14\n \\qquad\\text{for every representation }\\pi\\text{ of }C.$$ Then there are positive contractions $$p_1\\in\\operatorname{Her}(a),\\qquad q_1\\in\\operatorname{Her}(y)$$ such that $p_1$ is rich, $$\\label{eq:shrinking-signals}\n \\begin{split}\n  \\|\\pi(a)\\|>1/2&\\ \\Longrightarrow\\ \\|\\pi(p_1)\\|=1,\\\\\n  \\|\\pi(y)\\|>1/2&\\ \\Longrightarrow\\ \\|\\pi(q_1)\\|=1\n \\end{split}\n \\qquad\\text{for every representation }\\pi\\text{ of }C,$$ and $$\\label{eq:shrinking-support}\n \\|e_{p_1}c e_{q_1}\\|_{C^{**}}<1.$$ The last inequality is a single norm bound in the universal bidual.",
        "source_id": "PM-303.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-conditional-shrinking",
          "exact_quote": "Let $C$ be a $C^*$-algebra, let $a,y\\in C_+$ be contractions with $a$ rich, and let $c\\in\\widetilde C$ be a contraction. Suppose $p\\in\\operatorname{Her}(a)$ is a rich positive contraction satisfying $$\\|\\pi(a)\\|>1/2\\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\\|\\pi(p)\\|=1\n \\qquad\\text{for every representation }\\pi\\text{ of }C.$$ Put $d=y(1-c^*p^2c)y\\in C_+$, and assume $$\\label{eq:common-nonvanishing}\n \\pi(d)=0\\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\\|\\pi(y)\\|\\le\\frac14\n \\qquad\\text{for every representation }\\pi\\text{ of }C.$$ Then there are positive contractions $$p_1\\in\\operatorname{Her}(a),\\qquad q_1\\in\\operatorname{Her}(y)$$ such that $p_1$ is rich, $$\\label{eq:shrinking-signals}\n \\begin{split}\n  \\|\\pi(a)\\|>1/2&\\ \\Longrightarrow\\ \\|\\pi(p_1)\\|=1,\\\\\n  \\|\\pi(y)\\|>1/2&\\ \\Longrightarrow\\ \\|\\pi(q_1)\\|=1\n \\end{split}\n \\qquad\\text{for every representation }\\pi\\text{ of }C,$$ and $$\\label{eq:shrinking-support}\n \\|e_{p_1}c e_{q_1}\\|_{C^{**}}<1.$$ The last inequality is a single norm bound in the universal bidual.",
          "source_id": "PM-303.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $C$ be a $C^*$-algebra, let $a,y\\in C_+$ be contractions with $a$ rich, and let $c\\in\\widetilde C$ be a contraction.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-forbidden-tuple",
        "exact_quote": "There are $\\ell\\ge1$, a $C^*$-algebra $W$, contractions $z_1,\\ldots,z_\\ell\\in W$, and contractions $z_j^{(r)}\\in M_{N_r}(\\mathbb C)$, where $N_r\\ge1$, $r\\ge1$, and $1\\le j\\le\\ell$, with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *No tuple $b_1,\\ldots,b_\\ell$ in any exact $C^*$-algebra satisfies $$\\label{eq:forbidden-tuple}\n     \\left\\|\\sum_{j=1}^{\\ell} A_j\\otimes b_j\\right\\|\n     =\\left\\|\\sum_{j=1}^{\\ell} A_j\\otimes z_j\\right\\|\n     \\quad\\text{for every }m\\ge1\\text{ and every }A_j\\in M_m(\\mathbb C).$$*\n\n\n2.  *For every fixed $m\\ge1$ and $A_j\\in M_m(\\mathbb C)$, $$\\label{eq:tuple-approximations}\n     \\lim_{r\\to\\infty}\n     \\left\\|\\sum_{j=1}^{\\ell} A_j\\otimes z_j^{(r)}\\right\\|\n     =\\left\\|\\sum_{j=1}^{\\ell} A_j\\otimes z_j\\right\\|.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-303.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-forbidden-tuple",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-uniform-gap",
        "exact_quote": "There is a constant $\\gamma>0$ such that no unital exact $C^*$-algebra $B$ contains a contraction $v$ with the following property: for every integer $d\\geq1$ and every contraction $D\\in M_d(\\mathbb C)$, there are a unital representation $\\pi:B\\to B(H_\\pi)$ and an isometry $J:\\mathbb C^d\\to H_\\pi$ satisfying $$\\label{eq:forbidden-models}\n \\|\\pi(v)J-JD\\|<\\gamma,\n \\qquad\n \\|\\pi(v)^*J-JD^*\\|<\\gamma.$$ The representation and the isometry are allowed to depend on $d$ and $D$.",
        "source_id": "PM-303.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-uniform-gap",
          "exact_quote": "There is a constant $\\gamma>0$ such that no unital exact $C^*$-algebra $B$ contains a contraction $v$ with the following property: for every integer $d\\geq1$ and every contraction $D\\in M_d(\\mathbb C)$, there are a unital representation $\\pi:B\\to B(H_\\pi)$ and an isometry $J:\\mathbb C^d\\to H_\\pi$ satisfying $$\\label{eq:forbidden-models}\n \\|\\pi(v)J-JD\\|<\\gamma,\n \\qquad\n \\|\\pi(v)^*J-JD^*\\|<\\gamma.$$ The representation and the isometry are allowed to depend on $d$ and $D$.",
          "source_id": "PM-303.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There is a constant $\\gamma>0$ such that no unital exact $C^*$-algebra $B$ contains a contraction $v$ with the following property: for every integer $d\\geq1$ and every contraction $D\\in M_d(\\mathbb C)$, there are a unital representation $\\pi:B\\to B(H_\\pi)$ and an isometry $J:\\mathbb C^d\\to H_\\pi$ satisfying $$\\label{eq:forbidden-models}\n \\|\\pi(v)J-JD\\|<\\gamma,\n \\qquad\n \\|\\pi(v)^*J-JD^*\\|<\\gamma.$$ The representation and the isometry are allowed to depend on $d$ and $D$.",
      "explanation": "A universal positive error threshold obstructs approximately reducing copies of all finite matrix contractions inside any fixed contraction in a unital exact algebra. Both the contraction and its adjoint must use the same copy.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-uniform-matrix-bound",
        "exact_quote": "Let $E\\subset\\mathcal U$ be a finite-dimensional linear space consisting of $*$-polynomials in $u,u^*$. There is a finite constant $C_E\\geq0$ with the following property. Suppose $v$ is a contraction in a unital $C^*$-algebra $B$, and fix one number $\\theta>0$. Assume that for every integer $d\\geq1$ and every contraction $D\\in M_d(\\mathbb C)$, there are a unital representation $\\pi:B\\to B(H_\\pi)$ and an isometry $J:\\mathbb C^d\\to H_\\pi$ satisfying the two inequalities in <a href=\"#eq:forbidden-models\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:forbidden-models\">[eq:forbidden-models]</a> with $\\theta$ in place of $\\gamma$. For the homomorphism $\\Phi:\\mathcal U\\to B$ with $\\Phi(u)=v$, one then has $$\\label{eq:all-amplification-bound}\n \\|\\Phi^{(r)}(Z)\\|\\geq(1-C_E\\theta)\\|Z\\|\n \\qquad (r\\geq1,\\ Z\\in M_r(E)).$$",

      },

        "exact_quote": "Let $C$ be a $C^*$-algebra, let $h\\in C_+$, and let $n\\geq1$ be an integer such that $h^{\\oplus n}$ is properly infinite.         "anchor": "stmt-prop-perturbation",
        "exact_quote": "Let $C$ be an exact $C^*$-algebra satisfying <span class=\"upright\">(F$_n$)</span> for one fixed integer $n\\geq1$. Let $p,q\\in C$ be contractions and let $h,k,c\\in C_+$ satisfy $$p^*ph=h,\\qquad qq^*k=k,\\qquad\n c^{\\oplus n}\\precsim h,\\qquad c^{\\oplus n}\\precsim k.\n \\label{eq:perturbation-hypotheses}$$ For every $T\\in C$ and every $\\eta,\\alpha>0$, there is a contraction $y\\in C$ such that $$\\|(T+\\eta pyq)_P\\|\\geq\\sigma\\eta\n \\quad\\text{for every }P\\in\\operatorname{Prim}(C)\\text{ with }\\|c_P\\|\\geq\\alpha.\n \\label{eq:perturbation-conclusion}$$ If $h,k$ belong to a hereditary subalgebra $\\mathcal D\\subset C$, then $y$ may be chosen in $\\mathcal D$. If, in addition or independently, $p,q$ are positive and $pq=0$, then $y$ may be chosen selfadjoint. Both conclusions may be imposed simultaneously.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-local-common-element",
        "exact_quote": "Let $C$ be an exact $C^*$-algebra satisfying <span class=\"upright\">(F$_n$)</span> for one fixed integer $n\\geq1$, and let $P\\in\\operatorname{Prim}(C)$.*\n\n1.  *If $h\\in C_+$ and $h_P\\ne0$, then for every integer $m\\ge1$ there are $m$ pairwise orthogonal elements of $\\operatorname{Her}(h)_+$, each nonzero at $P$.*\n\n2.  *If $h,k\\in C_+$ are nonzero at $P$, there is $c\\in C_+$ such that $c_P\\ne0$ and $$c^{\\oplus n}\\precsim h,\\qquad c^{\\oplus n}\\precsim k.$$ If $h,k$ belong to a hereditary subalgebra $\\mathcal D$, $c$ can also be chosen in $\\mathcal D$.",

        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $C$ be an exact $C^*$-algebra satisfying <span class=\"upright\">(F$_n$)</span> for one fixed integer $n\\geq1$, and let $P\\in\\operatorname{Prim}(C)$.*\n\n1.  *If $h\\in C_+$ and $h_P\\ne0$, then for every integer $m\\ge1$ there are $m$ pairwise orthogonal elements of $\\operatorname{Her}(h)_+$, each nonzero at $P$.*\n\n2.  *If $h,k\\in C_+$ are nonzero at $P$, there is $c\\in C_+$ such that $c_P\\ne0$ and $$c^{\\oplus n}\\precsim h,\\qquad c^{\\oplus n}\\precsim k.$$ If $h,k$ belong to a hereditary subalgebra $\\mathcal D$, $c$ can also be chosen in $\\mathcal D$.",

        "exact_quote": "Let $C$ be an exact $C^*$-algebra satisfying <span class=\"upright\">(F$_n$)</span> for one fixed integer $n\\geq1$.         "exact_quote": "Let $C$ be an exact $C^*$-algebra satisfying $\\textup{(P)}$.       },

          "source_id": "PM-303.1"
        }

<!-- PM:END pm.claims.v2 -->

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Stable Cuntz comparison and equivalence** ([source](#pm-h-3)): For $u,v\in(C\otimes\mathcal K)_+$, write $u\precsim v$ if some sequence $x_j\in C\otimes\mathcal K$ satisfies $x_j^*vx_j\to u$ in norm, and write $u\sim_{\mathrm{Cu}}v$ if comparison holds in both directions.
- **Finite direct sum amplification** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Write $h^{\oplus n}$ for the direct sum of $n$ copies of $h$.
- **Properly infinite positive element** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A positive element $h$ is *properly infinite* if $h\oplus h\precsim h$.
- **Hereditary subalgebra and support projection** ([source](#sec:foundations)): For a $C^*$-algebra $D$ and $h\in D_+$, let $\operatorname{Id}_D(h)$ denote the closed two-sided ideal generated by $h$, and put $$\operatorname{Her}_D(h)=\overline{hDh},\qquad e_h=\mathbf 1_{(0,\infty)}(h)\in D^{**}.$$
- **Individual proper infiniteness condition** ([source](#eq:SPI-definition)): Condition $\textup{(P)}$ is equivalent to pure infiniteness in the nonsimple sense of Kirchberg–Rørdam (Kirchberg and Rørdam 2000, Theorem 4.16) [[KR2000](#ref-KR2000)]; the equivalence is also recalled in (Schafhauser, Tikuisis, and White 2026, sec. 21) [[STW99problems](#ref-STW99problems)].
- **Fixed-amplification weak pure infiniteness** ([source](#eq:SPI-definition)): The existence of an $n$ satisfying $\textup{(F\ensuremath{_n})}$ is *weak pure infiniteness* in the fixed-amplification sense.
- **Strong pure infiniteness** ([source](#eq:SPI-definition)): We use the following formulation of *strong pure infiniteness*, valid for arbitrary $C^*$-algebras (Kirchberg and Sierakowski 2016, Definition 3.1) [[KS](#ref-KS)]: for every $x,y\in C_+$ and $\varepsilon>0$, there are $s,t\in C$ such that $$\label{eq:SPI-definition} \\|s^*x^2s-x^2\\|<\varepsilon,\qquad \\|t^*y^2t-y^2\\|<\varepsilon,\qquad \\|s^*xy t\\|<\varepsilon.$$
- **Rich positive contraction** ([source](#stmt-def-rich)): A positive contraction $a$ is *rich* if $(a-\varepsilon)_+$ is properly infinite for every $\varepsilon\ge0$.
- **Ramp** ([source](#sec:foundations)): A *ramp* is a continuous nondecreasing function with values in $[0,1]$, zero on a neighborhood of zero and constant at $1$ beyond a specified larger threshold.
- **Minimal unitization convention** ([source](#sec:graph)): For a $C^*$-algebra $C$, write $\widetilde C$ for its minimal unitization when $C$ is nonunital, and set $\widetilde C=C$ when $C$ is unital.
- **Exact algebra** ([source](#pm-h-15)): Recall that a $C^*$-algebra $D$ is *exact* if tensoring every short exact sequence with $D$ in the minimal tensor norm preserves exactness.
- **Universal contraction algebra** ([source](#pm-h-20)): Let $\mathcal U=C^*(1,u)$ be the universal unital $C^*$-algebra generated by a contraction.
- **Primitive norm slices** ([source](#stmt-lem-primitive-compactness)): For $c\in C_+$ and $\alpha>0$, the set $$\Omega(c,\alpha)=\{P\in\operatorname{Prim}(C):\\|c_P\\|>\alpha\}$$ is open, and $$K(c,\alpha)=\{P\in\operatorname{Prim}(C):\\|c_P\\|\ge\alpha\}$$ is quasi-compact.
- **Annihilator central-sequence quotient** ([source](#pm-h-18)): Let $C_\omega$ be the norm ultrapower of $C$ along a free ultrafilter on $\mathbb N$, with $C$ embedded diagonally, and put $$F(C)=\frac{C_\omega\cap C'}{\operatorname{Ann}(C,C_\omega)}, \qquad \operatorname{Ann}(C,C_\omega) =\{x\in C_\omega:xC=Cx=0\}.$$

| q-graph-coefficient-budget | \gamma_g^2+k\sigma_g^2 | < | $1$ | The coordinate count and coefficients are fixed before the measurements. |
| q-product-row-ceiling | \\|s\\| | ≤ | $\delta^{-1/2}$ | The cutoff determines a common bound across all product coordinates. |
| q-uniform-support-factor | \theta | < | $1$ | The same factor works for all algebras and inputs in the stated regime. |
| q-iterated-compression-rate | \\|e_{p_{k+1}}c_*e_{q_{k+1}}\\| | ≤ | $\theta^{k+1}$ | The factor is existential; the iteration preserves both original representation signals. |
| q-bounded-diagonal-error | \\|s^*as-a\\|,\ \\|t^*bt-b\\| | < | $2\delta+\rho$ | Diagonal accuracy is governed by the cutoff and comparison error. |
| q-diagonalization-coefficient-ceiling | \\|s\\|,\ \\|t\\| | ≤ | $\delta^{-1/2}$ | The bound is independent of the requested mixed-entry accuracy. |
| q-universal-exactness-error-gap | \gamma | > | $0$ | The gap is common to all unital exact algebras and matrix sizes. |
| q-polynomial-space-evaluation-floor | \\|\Phi^{(r)}(Z)\\| | ≥ | $(1-C_E\theta)\\|Z\\|$ | The constant depends on the fixed polynomial space and representatives, uniformly over matrix amplifications. |
| q-polynomial-space-constant | C_E | = | $\sum_{i=1}^k\\|f_i\\|\sum_w\|a_{iw}\|\,\|w\|$ | Coordinate functional norms, polynomial coefficients, and word lengths determine the displayed constant. |
| q-perturbation-constant-window | \sigma | < | $\min\{1,\gamma/3\}$ | The positive constant is fixed independently of the algebra and amplification integer. |
| q-primitive-perturbation-floor | \\|(T+\eta pyq)_P\\| | ≥ | $\sigma\eta$ | The floor scales with the perturbation size; its proportionality constant is independent of all input data. |
| q-finite-coupling-retained-floor | \\|(p_iUq_i)_P\\| | > | $\frac{\sigma\eta_i}{2}$ | The geometric perturbation schedule leaves an earlier corner nonzero after all later stages. |
| q-separated-positive-fixed-floor | m | = | $\frac{r\sigma^2}{1024}$ | The floor depends on the prescribed norm threshold and the universal perturbation constant, independently of the later product parameter. |
| q-separated-positive-product | \\|Q_aQ_b\\| | ≤ | $20\sqrt\ell$ | The product parameter is chosen after the quotient norm floor. |
| q-spectral-floor-implementer-ceiling | \\|x\\| | ≤ | $\frac4m$ | The coefficient bound is independent of the original comparison implementer. |
| q-implemented-mixed-product | \\|x_a^*Q_aQ_bx_b\\| | ≤ | $\frac{320\sqrt\ell}{m^2}$ | The fixed coefficient floor allows the later product parameter to control the implemented mixed entry. |

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-uniform-gap",
          "exact_quote": "There is a constant $\\gamma>0$ such that no unital exact $C^*$-algebra $B$ contains a contraction $v$ with the following property: for every integer $d\\geq1$ and every contraction $D\\in M_d(\\mathbb C)$, there are a unital representation $\\pi:B\\to B(H_\\pi)$ and an isometry $J:\\mathbb C^d\\to H_\\pi$ satisfying $$\\label{eq:forbidden-models}\n \\|\\pi(v)J-JD\\|<\\gamma,\n \\qquad\n \\|\\pi(v)^*J-JD^*\\|<\\gamma.$$ The representation and the isometry are allowed to depend on $d$ and $D$.",
          "source_id": "PM-303.1"
        }
      ],

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-uniform-matrix-bound",
          "exact_quote": "Let $E\\subset\\mathcal U$ be a finite-dimensional linear space consisting of $*$-polynomials in $u,u^*$. There is a finite constant $C_E\\geq0$ with the following property. Suppose $v$ is a contraction in a unital $C^*$-algebra $B$, and fix one number $\\theta>0$. Assume that for every integer $d\\geq1$ and every contraction $D\\in M_d(\\mathbb C)$, there are a unital representation $\\pi:B\\to B(H_\\pi)$ and an isometry $J:\\mathbb C^d\\to H_\\pi$ satisfying the two inequalities in <a href=\"#eq:forbidden-models\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:forbidden-models\">[eq:forbidden-models]</a> with $\\theta$ in place of $\\gamma$. For the homomorphism $\\Phi:\\mathcal U\\to B$ with $\\Phi(u)=v$, one then has $$\\label{eq:all-amplification-bound}\n \\|\\Phi^{(r)}(Z)\\|\\geq(1-C_E\\theta)\\|Z\\|\n \\qquad (r\\geq1,\\ Z\\in M_r(E)).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-303.1"
        }

          "exact_quote": "If $E=0$, take $C_E=0$. Otherwise choose a basis $P_1,\\ldots,P_k$ of $E$, with coordinate functionals $f_1,\\ldots,f_k$, and choose polynomial representatives $P_i=\\sum_w a_{iw}w$. Define $$\\label{eq:explicit-CE}\n C_E=\\sum_{i=1}^k\\|f_i\\|\\sum_w|a_{iw}|\\,|w|.$$ Constants are allowed in the representatives; the empty word has length zero. For $Z=\\sum_i B_i\\otimes P_i\\in M_r(E)$ and scalar unit vectors $\\alpha,\\beta\\in\\mathbb C^r$, $$|\\alpha^*B_i\\beta|\n =|f_i(\\alpha^*Z\\beta)|\\leq\\|f_i\\|\\|Z\\|.$$ It follows that $\\|B_i\\|\\leq\\|f_i\\|\\|Z\\|$ for every matrix size $r$.",
          "source_id": "PM-303.1"
        }

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-comparison-rules** [claim](#stmt-lem-comparison-rules) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-comparison-rules): Hereditary support identification, rectangular coefficients, approximation, and proper-target ideal comparison. -> Comparison rules for hereditary elements, block sums, limits, homomorphisms, and generated ideals.
- **step-cutoff-transport** [claim](#stmt-lem-transport) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transport): Compress a comparison coefficient, apply a cutoff, and take a rectangular polar decomposition in the bidual. -> A common carrier preserving algebra membership, hereditary images, cutoffs, and represented norms.
- **step-ideal-cutoffs** [claim](#stmt-lem-ideals-cutoffs) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ideals-cutoffs): Quotient functional calculus, irreducible separation, and equality of support projections. -> Cutoff ideal exhaustion, primitive ideal detection, and common-support comparison equivalence.
- **step-graph-measurements** [claim](#stmt-prop-graph) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-graph): Build a graph in orthogonal transported copies and recover its entries from equality in contraction inequalities. -> Fixed measurements whose simultaneous equality is forced by defect vanishing.
- **step-conditional-shrinking** [claim](#stmt-lem-conditional-shrinking) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-conditional-shrinking): Convert representation nonvanishing into ideal inclusions, take spectral cutoffs, and estimate the universal support norm. -> Strict mixed support compression retaining both represented norm signals.
- **step-actual-range** [claim](#stmt-lem-actual-range) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-actual-range): Use a bounded inverse on the spectral cutoff and positivity of the operator product. -> Actual-range inclusion and trivial kernel intersection.
- **step-four-entry-avoidance** [claim](#stmt-prop-four-avoidance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-four-avoidance): Directed finite tests, transported entries, a norm ultraproduct, and a scaling-element defect contradiction. -> An entry choice avoiding simultaneous represented measurement equality.
- **step-individual-shrinking** [claim](#stmt-prop-shrinking-p) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-shrinking-p): Insert the avoiding entries into the graph and apply conditional shrinking. -> Hereditary corners with strict mixed support compression and represented norm retention.
- **step-product-permanence** [claim](#stmt-lem-product-p) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-product-p): Construct cutoff comparison rows in each coordinate with a common norm ceiling. -> Individual proper infiniteness in the bounded product.
- **step-uniform-support-contraction** [claim](#stmt-lem-uniform-shrinking) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-uniform-shrinking): Contradict the failure of a universal bound by shrinking inputs in their bounded product. -> A universal support contraction factor.
- **step-iterated-separation** [claim](#stmt-prop-iterated-separation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-iterated-separation): Normalize the compressed mixed entry and repeatedly shrink nested hereditary corners. -> Arbitrarily small support compression with both original norm signals retained.
- **step-bounded-diagonalization** [claim](#stmt-prop-bounded-diagonalization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-bounded-diagonalization): Separate high spectral corners first, compare diagonal cutoffs into them, and bound the coefficients by the diagonal spectral floor. -> Simultaneous diagonal approximation and small mixed entry with bounded coefficients.
- **step-general-strong-infiniteness** [claim](#stmt-thm-individual-to-strong) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-bounded-diagonalization-2): Scale arbitrary positive inputs to contractions and specialize arbitrary-entry diagonalization to the defining mixed product. -> Strong pure infiniteness for arbitrary complex algebras satisfying individual proper infiniteness.
- **step-forbidden-tuple** [claim](#stmt-lem-forbidden-tuple) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-forbidden-tuple): Transfer a nonexact tensor quotient gap through joint matrix norms and approximate the tuple by finite compressions. -> A finite tuple forbidden in exact algebras and pointwise finite matrix approximations.
- **step-nuclear-avoidance** [claim](#stmt-prop-nuclear-avoidance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-nuclear-avoidance): Transport every entry through a common carrier and pass fixed measurements to a quotient of the matrix algebra. -> An entry choice that excludes the graph failure relations.
- **step-nuclear-shrinking** [claim](#stmt-prop-shrinking-nuclear) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-shrinking-nuclear): Use nuclear avoidance in the graph, then convert nonvanishing into strict support compression. -> Strict hereditary support compression retaining the representation signals.
- **step-local-rich-elements** [claim](#stmt-lem-local-rich) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-local-rich): Exclude finite-rank hereditary images, apply Kadison transitivity, find a common comparison source, and transport its amplified cutoff. -> A local rich hereditary contraction with represented norm at its plateau.
- **step-rich-merger** [claim](#stmt-lem-merger) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-merger): Shrink successive transported copies, use polar deformation to enforce orthogonality, and sum the final orthogonal family. -> A rich contraction retaining the union of the input norm signals.
- **step-nuclear-amplification-removal** [claim](#stmt-thm-nuclear-to-individual) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-merger-2): Exhaust the generated ideal by finitely many local cutoff witnesses, merge them, compare the doubled cutoff, and take a norm limit. -> Individual proper infiniteness under nuclearity and the fixed-amplification hypothesis.
- **step-nuclear-absorption** [claim](#stmt-cor-absorption) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rem-tuple-exact): Apply strong pure infiniteness and the external central-sequence embedding and absorption criteria through the annihilator quotient. -> Tensorial absorption in the general nonunital separable nuclear regime.
- **step-universal-nonexactness** [claim](#stmt-lem-nonexact-universal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-nonexact-universal): Embed the full free-group algebra and contrast a tensor quotient representation with its spatial tree-adjacency norm. -> Nonexactness of the universal contraction algebra.
- **step-finite-matrix-tests** [claim](#stmt-lem-finite-matrix-tests) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-matrix-tests): Compress to the span of all word suffixes applied to a near-norming matrix test vector. -> Its norm as a supremum over finite matrix contractions.
- **step-uniform-polynomial-bound** [claim](#stmt-lem-uniform-matrix-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-uniform-matrix-bound): Bound coordinate matrices uniformly and telescope words using both approximate intertwining relations. -> An evaluation lower bound uniform over every amplification.
- **step-universal-gap** [claim](#stmt-prop-uniform-gap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-uniform-matrix-bound-2): Hold a polynomial-space evaluation fixed across tensor compressions and an ideal approximate-unit net; derive injectivity of the forbidden tensor quotient. -> A positive universal gap for approximately reducing finite matrix models in exact targets.
- **step-amplified-ideal-comparison** [claim](#stmt-lem-ideal-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ideal-comparison): Identify the stabilized generated ideal and compare into its properly infinite amplified target. -> Comparison of every finite source amplification into the fixed target amplification.
- **step-exact-cut-implementation** [claim](#stmt-lem-cut-implementation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cut-implementation): Multiply a cutoff transport carrier by the square root of the continuous cut function. -> An algebra-valued rectangular coefficient with exact initial square and hereditary range square.
- **step-exact-unital-matrix-target** [claim](#stmt-lem-exact-matrix-unitization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-exact-matrix-unitization): Use finite matrix tensor identifications and split the unitized tensor product through its scalar character. -> Exactness of its unitized finite matrix amplification.
- **step-uniform-primitive-perturbation** [claim](#stmt-prop-perturbation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-perturbation): Fix comparison rows and a clipped global matrix contraction before testing finite models; failed perturbations would violate the universal gap. -> A simultaneous quotient norm floor with hereditary and selfadjoint options.
- **step-local-common-elements** [claim](#stmt-lem-local-common-element) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-local-common-element): Exclude finite-rank positive images, use strong density to select orthogonal positives, and repeatedly form common comparison sources. -> Local orthogonal lists and a common positive element comparing with the required multiplicity.
- **step-pure-state-norm-attainment** [claim](#stmt-lem-purestateattainment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-purestateattainment): Extend a norm-attaining spectral state, choose an extreme point of its compact face, and restrict its irreducible representation to the original algebra. -> An irreducible representation attaining its norm.
- **step-primitive-slice-compactness** [claim](#stmt-lem-primitive-compactness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-primitive-compactness): Express open sets by ideals, attain the quotient norm to exclude an uncovered point, and approximate inside a finite ideal sum. -> Openness of strict slices and quasi-compactness of non-strict slices.
- **step-finite-coupling** [claim](#stmt-lem-finite-coupling) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-coupling): Select a finite primitive cover and apply geometrically decreasing selfadjoint hereditary perturbations. -> A common selfadjoint coupling with a selected nonzero corner at every covered point.
- **step-square-zero-localization** [claim](#stmt-prop-square-zero) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-square-zero): Couple local orthogonal positives, couple separated spectral windows, delete narrow diagonal blocks, and finally couple both signs. -> A square-zero hereditary element nonzero at every point of the subset.
- **step-exact-amplification-removal** [claim](#stmt-thm-exact-infiniteness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-square-zero-2): Repeat square-zero halving on the same norm slice, accumulate enough equivalent copies, compare the doubled cutoff into the original input, and take a norm limit. -> Individual proper infiniteness and then strong pure infiniteness.
- **step-whole-ideal-comparison** [claim](#stmt-cor-ideal-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-ideal-comparison): Use the properly infinite scalar generator as the target of amplified ideal comparison. -> Whole-ideal comparison and availability of the exact perturbation theorem at scalar multiplicity.
- **step-fixed-floor-positive-separation** [claim](#stmt-lem-separating-positives) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-separating-positives): Separate low singular values, construct orthogonal high cores, and apply compatible inverse perturbations while controlling all cross products. -> Hereditary positives with a retained quotient floor and a small mutual product.
- **step-bounded-spectral-implementation** [claim](#stmt-lem-bounded-implementation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-bounded-implementation): Clip comparison through a spectral floor and estimate the implementer by its implemented square. -> Bounded scalar comparison coefficients.
- **step-direct-exact-strong-infiniteness** [claim](#stmt-prop-direct-strong-infiniteness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-direct-strong-infiniteness): Choose the quotient floor before product accuracy, implement the separated positives with bounded coefficients, and approximate by left multiples of the original inputs. -> The defining diagonal and mixed inequalities for strong pure infiniteness.

In particular, the ideals $\\operatorname{Id}((z-\\delta)_+)$, directed as $\\delta\\downarrow0$, densely exhaust $\\operatorname{Id}(z)$.*\n\n2.  *For $u,v\\in D_+$, one has $u\\in\\operatorname{Id}(v)$ if and only if every irreducible representation $\\pi$ with $\\pi(v)=0$ also satisfies $\\pi(u)=0$.*\n\n3.  *If $f,g$ are continuous nonnegative functions of a positive element $a$, vanish at zero, and have the same open positivity set in $\\operatorname{sp}(a)$, then $\\operatorname{Her}(f(a))=\\operatorname{Her}(g(a))$ and $f(a)\\sim_{\\mathrm{Cu}}g(a)$.",

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-graph",
          "exact_quote": "Let $C$ be a $C^*$-algebra, let $a,y\\in C_+$ be contractions with $a$ rich, and let $c\\in\\widetilde C$ be a contraction. For an integer $k\\ge1$ and fixed positive $\\gamma_g,\\sigma_g$ satisfying <a href=\"#eq:graph-constants\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:graph-constants\">[eq:graph-constants]</a>, there are $$X\\in\\operatorname{Her}((a-1/8)_+)_+,\\qquad\n B_1,\\ldots,B_k\\in\\operatorname{Her}((a-1/8)_+)$$ with the following property. For any contractions $$S_i\\in\\operatorname{Her}((X-\\alpha_0)_+),\\qquad 1\\le i\\le k,$$ one can form a rich positive contraction $p\\in\\operatorname{Her}(a)$ such that, for every representation $\\pi$ of $C$, $\\|\\pi(a)\\|>1/2$ implies $\\|\\pi(p)\\|=1$. If a representation $\\pi$ of $C$ satisfies $$\\pi\\bigl(y(1-c^*p^2c)y\\bigr)=0,\\qquad \\|\\pi(y)\\|>s,$$ then, in that same representation, $$\\label{eq:graph-failure}\n \\|\\pi(X)\\|>s,\\qquad \\pi(B_i)=\\pi(S_i)\\quad(1\\le i\\le k).$$ The elements $X,B_1,\\ldots,B_k$ are fixed before the $S_i$ are chosen.",
          "source_id": "PM-303.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-graph-measurements",
      "inputs": [
        "A rich positive contraction",
        "A second positive contraction and a unitized mixed contraction",
        "Graph coefficients fixed before entry choices"
      ],
      "method": "Build a graph in orthogonal transported copies and recover its entries from equality in contraction inequalities.",
      "output": "Fixed measurements whose simultaneous equality is forced by defect vanishing.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-prop-graph"

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-conditional-shrinking",
          "exact_quote": "Let $C$ be a $C^*$-algebra, let $a,y\\in C_+$ be contractions with $a$ rich, and let $c\\in\\widetilde C$ be a contraction. Suppose $p\\in\\operatorname{Her}(a)$ is a rich positive contraction satisfying $$\\|\\pi(a)\\|>1/2\\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\\|\\pi(p)\\|=1\n \\qquad\\text{for every representation }\\pi\\text{ of }C.$$ Put $d=y(1-c^*p^2c)y\\in C_+$, and assume $$\\label{eq:common-nonvanishing}\n \\pi(d)=0\\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\\|\\pi(y)\\|\\le\\frac14\n \\qquad\\text{for every representation }\\pi\\text{ of }C.$$ Then there are positive contractions $$p_1\\in\\operatorname{Her}(a),\\qquad q_1\\in\\operatorname{Her}(y)$$ such that $p_1$ is rich, $$\\label{eq:shrinking-signals}\n \\begin{split}\n  \\|\\pi(a)\\|>1/2&\\ \\Longrightarrow\\ \\|\\pi(p_1)\\|=1,\\\\\n  \\|\\pi(y)\\|>1/2&\\ \\Longrightarrow\\ \\|\\pi(q_1)\\|=1\n \\end{split}\n \\qquad\\text{for every representation }\\pi\\text{ of }C,$$ and $$\\label{eq:shrinking-support}\n \\|e_{p_1}c e_{q_1}\\|_{C^{**}}<1.$$ The last inequality is a single norm bound in the universal bidual.",
          "source_id": "PM-303.1"
        }

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-forbidden-tuple",
          "exact_quote": "There are $\\ell\\ge1$, a $C^*$-algebra $W$, contractions $z_1,\\ldots,z_\\ell\\in W$, and contractions $z_j^{(r)}\\in M_{N_r}(\\mathbb C)$, where $N_r\\ge1$, $r\\ge1$, and $1\\le j\\le\\ell$, with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *No tuple $b_1,\\ldots,b_\\ell$ in any exact $C^*$-algebra satisfies $$\\label{eq:forbidden-tuple}\n     \\left\\|\\sum_{j=1}^{\\ell} A_j\\otimes b_j\\right\\|\n     =\\left\\|\\sum_{j=1}^{\\ell} A_j\\otimes z_j\\right\\|\n     \\quad\\text{for every }m\\ge1\\text{ and every }A_j\\in M_m(\\mathbb C).$$*\n\n\n2.  *For every fixed $m\\ge1$ and $A_j\\in M_m(\\mathbb C)$, $$\\label{eq:tuple-approximations}\n     \\lim_{r\\to\\infty}\n     \\left\\|\\sum_{j=1}^{\\ell} A_j\\otimes z_j^{(r)}\\right\\|\n     =\\left\\|\\sum_{j=1}^{\\ell} A_j\\otimes z_j\\right\\|.$$",

          "exact_quote": "Let $E\\subset\\mathcal U$ be a finite-dimensional linear space consisting of $*$-polynomials in $u,u^*$. There is a finite constant $C_E\\geq0$ with the following property. <a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **individual-to-simultaneous-comparison**: Individual proper infiniteness of every positive element implies simultaneous diagonal approximation with an arbitrary mixed entry, and hence strong pure infiniteness.
- **exact-fixed-amplification-removal**: Exactness and a single amplification integer valid for every positive element imply individual proper infiniteness and strong pure infiniteness.
- **separable-nuclear-absorption**: The fixed-amplification property yields absorption of the Cuntz algebra in the separable nuclear setting, including nonunital algebras.
- **transport-membership-and-norms**: Cutoff transport preserves hereditary algebra structure and represented norms; its regularized products belong to the algebra even though the carrier itself is only asserted to belong to the bidual.
- **primitive-slice-localization**: A hereditary subalgebra nonzero throughout a prescribed quasi-compact primitive set contains a square-zero element nonzero throughout that same set.
- **bounded-diagonal-implementation**: For positive contraction inputs, comparison coefficients have a norm ceiling determined by the diagonal cutoff parameters, independent of the requested mixed-entry accuracy.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **nonexact-amplification-removal**: Remove the fixed amplification in an arbitrary nonexact algebra. -> The amplification-removal conclusion has not been established at that scope; the general ordinary-to-strong theorem requires individual proper infiniteness already.
- **element-dependent-amplification**: Choose a different amplification integer for each positive element and invoke fixed-amplification removal. -> An element-dependent choice does not establish a common amplification valid for every scalar positive element and its later cutoffs.
- **absorption-outside-separable-nuclear-scope**: Infer tensorial absorption from strong pure infiniteness without establishing separability and nuclearity. -> The absorption consequence is stated for separable nuclear algebras; its central-sequence interface does not supply this conclusion at arbitrary scope.
- **bidual-carrier-as-algebra-element**: Treat the cutoff transport carrier itself as an element of the original rectangular algebra. -> Only specified products with the cutoff hereditary algebra have algebra membership; the carrier is asserted to lie in the bidual.
- **finite-tests-as-universal-certificate**: Certify the forbidden-tuple obstruction or universal matrix norms using a fixed finite collection of matrix tests. -> The obstruction quantifies over all matrix levels and coefficients; pointwise finite matrix approximations do not establish a finite decision procedure.
- **coordinatewise-product-comparison**: Infer comparison in a bounded product from coordinatewise comparison without uniform control. -> The coordinate coefficients may fail to define a bounded row in the product algebra.
- **quasicompact-means-closed**: Treat primitive norm slices as closed compact subsets of a Hausdorff space. -> The result supplies quasi-compactness without a Hausdorff assumption, and the proof does not assert closedness of the slice.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main results are existence statements for arbitrary operator algebras, involving stable norm-limit comparison, representations, bidual transport, and universal matrix tests. They do not specify a finite executable witness-selection procedure. The proof of the forbidden-tuple lemma explicitly states: "The conclusion excludes simultaneous equality for all finite matrix tests; it does not assert that a fixed finite list of tests suffices." The uniform support contraction is obtained by a product contradiction rather than a supplied numerical contraction factor. Finite matrix examples or scalar checks would therefore cover auxiliary special cases without deciding the stated invariants.
